respectively. Hence, the inductive hypothesis implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[Z^{(1)}_{1}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}^{(2)},\ldots,\mathbf{D}_{n}^{(m)}]^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq C^{m-1}\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\left(\frac{q}{k}\right)^{t}\Gamma_{t,q}(g_{0})\quad\text{and}

(A.26)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|Z^{(1)}_{1}|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq C^{m-1}\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\left(\frac{q}{k}\right)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\Gamma_{t,q}(g_{D^{(1)}_{i_{1}}})^{q}\right]^{1/q}\penalty\ .

By recognizing

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,q}(g_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[g(D_{1},\ldots,D_{m})\mid D_{2},\ldots,D_{t+1}]^{q}\right]^{1/q}=\Gamma_{t,q}(g)\quad\text{and}

(A.27)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Gamma_{t,q}(g_{D_{1}^{(1)}})^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[g(D_{1},\ldots,D_{m})\mid D_{1},\ldots,D_{t+1}]^{q}\right]^{1/q}=\Gamma_{t+1,q}(g)

for each
0\leq t\leq m-1
and plugging the bounds (
A.26
) into (
A.23
), we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|A_{m,k}(g)|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq C^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\left(\frac{q}{k}\right)^{t}\Gamma_{t,q}(g)+C^{m}\frac{q}{k}\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\left(\frac{q}{k}\right)^{t}\Gamma_{t+1,q}(g)

(A.28)

\displaystyle\leq C^{m}\sum_{t=0}^{m}\left(\frac{q}{k}\right)^{t}\Gamma_{t,q}(g)\penalty\ ,

as required.

A.2.2.
Part (ii)

We begin by stating two facts that will be applied at several points throughout the ensuing argument. First, we record the following property of sub-exponential random variables.

Lemma A.4

(
Vershynin, (2018)
, Proposition 2.7.1)
.

Let
Z
be a real-valued random variable. Then the moment inequality

(A.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{q}]^{1/q}\leq Cq\|Z\|_{\psi_{1}}

holds for each
q\geq 1
.

Second, we note that if
Z^{\prime}
is an independent copy of
Z
, then the triangle inequality implies that

(A.30)

\displaystyle\|Z-Z^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|Z\|_{\psi_{1}}+\|Z^{\prime}\|_{\psi_{1}}=2\|Z\|_{\psi_{1}}\penalty\ .

Thus, by Jensen’s inequality, we obtain the bound

(A.31)

\displaystyle\|f^{(t)}(D_{[t]})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\|\nabla_{1}\cdots\nabla_{t}f(D_{[b]})\|\leq 2^{t}\phi

for each integer
0\leq t\leq b
.

Now, our interest is in the quantity

(A.32)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,q}((f^{(m)})^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}[f^{(m)}(D_{[m]})^{2}\mid D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}]^{q}\right]^{1/q}

for each integer
0\leq t\leq m
. We first consider the case
0\leq t\leq m-1
. To this end, for each deterministic collection
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{t})
in
\mathcal{D}^{t}
, define the symmetric, order
b-t
kernel

\displaystyle g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}(d_{t+1},\ldots,d_{b})

(A.33)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{1}\cdots\nabla_{t}f(D_{[b]})\mid D_{1}=d_{1},\ldots,D_{t}=d_{t},D_{t+1}=d_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b}=d_{b}\right]

and let
g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}^{(1)},\ldots,g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}^{(b-t)}
denote its Hoeffding projections, as defined in Part (i) of
Lemma

2.1
. By Part (i) of
Lemma

2.1
, these projections are uncorrelated, and so the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{t+l})]^{2}

(A.34)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{b-t}{b-t\choose l}\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg(g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}^{(l)}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{t+l})\bigg)^{2}\right]

holds. Now, observe that

(A.35)

\displaystyle g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}^{(m-t)}(d_{t+1},\ldots,d_{m})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{t+1}\cdots\nabla_{m}g_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})\mid D_{t+1}=d_{t+1},\ldots,D_{m}=d_{m}\right]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{1}\cdots\nabla_{m}f(D_{[b]})\mid D_{1}=d_{1},\ldots,D_{m}=d_{m}\right]

(A.36)

\displaystyle=f^{(m)}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{m})\penalty\ ,

where the first and last equalities follow by definition and the second equality follows from the fact that the stochastic difference operators act on distinct coordinates, and thereby commute. As a consequence, the decomposition (
A.34
) implies that

\displaystyle{b-t\choose m-t}\mathbb{E}\left[f^{(m)}(D_{1},\ldots,D_{t},D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{m})^{2}\mid D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}\right]

(A.37)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[g_{D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})^{2}\mid D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}\right]

holds almost surely. Therefore, we obtain the bound

(A.38)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,q}((f^{(m)})^{2})

\displaystyle\leq{b-t\choose m-t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[g_{D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})^{2}\mid D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}\right]^{q}\right]^{1/q}\penalty\ .

Now, Jensen’s inequality for conditional expectation and
Lemma

A.4
imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[g_{D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})^{2}\mid D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}\right]^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|g_{D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})\right|^{2q}\right]^{1/q}

(A.39)

\displaystyle\leq(Cq)^{2}\|g_{D_{1},\ldots,D_{t}}(D_{t+1},\ldots,D_{b})\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}\leq(Cq)^{2}\phi^{2}4^{t}\penalty\ ,

where the last step follows from (
A.31
). Hence, as

(A.40)

\displaystyle{b-t\choose m-t}^{-1}\leq{b\choose m}^{-1}\left(\frac{eb}{m}\right)^{t}\penalty\ ,

we can conclude that

(A.41)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t,q}\bigl((f^{(m)})^{2}\bigr)\leq\phi^{2}(Cq)^{2}C^{t}{b\choose m}^{-1}\left(\frac{b}{m}\right)^{t}

for each
0\leq t\leq m-1
.

It remains to consider the case
t=m
. In this case, we have that

(A.42)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{m,q}\bigl((f^{(m)})^{2}\bigr)=\mathbb{E}[|f^{(m)}(D_{[m]})|^{2q}]^{1/q}\leq\phi^{2}(Cq)^{2}4^{m}\penalty\ .

by
Lemma

A.4
and (
A.31
). The bound
{b\choose m}\leq(eb/m)^{m}
then implies that

(A.43)

\displaystyle 4^{m}\leq C^{m}{b\choose m}^{-1}\left(\frac{b}{m}\right)^{m}\penalty\ ,

completing the proof.

A.3.
Proof of
Corollary

1.1

The result follows by considering each of the terms in the decomposition

\displaystyle U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]

(A.44)

\displaystyle=\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})+\left(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)\penalty\ .

The first quantity can be handled through the application of the following standard Bernstein-type bound, stated, e.g., in
Song et al., (2019)
.

Lemma A.5

(Lemma A.2,
Song et al., (2019)
)
.

Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
be independent,
centered, random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Define the quantity

(A.45)

\sigma^{2}=\max_{j\in[d]}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{2}_{i,j}\right]

and assume that
\|Z_{ij}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\phi
for all
i\in[n]
and
j\in[d]
.
The inequality

\displaystyle P\left\{\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\Big\|_{\infty}\geq C\left(\sigma\log^{1/2}(dg)+\phi\log(nd)\left(\log(nd)+\log(g)\right)\right)\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{g}

holds for any constant
g>0
.

In particular, observe that

(A.46)

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[d]}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{b^{2}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[u^{(1)}(D_{i})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{2}}{n}\overline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\frac{b}{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\frac{b}{n}\phi

Thus,
Lemma

A.5
implies that

(A.47)

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{b\overline{\sigma}_{b}}{n^{1/2}}\log^{1/2}(nd)+\phi\frac{b}{n}\log^{2}(nd)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
. In turn,
Theorem

1.1
implies that

(A.48)

\displaystyle\Big\|U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\phi\frac{b}{n}\log^{2}(nd)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
. Hence, we can conclude that

(A.49)

\displaystyle\Big\|U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]\Big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{b^{2}\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{b}\log(nd)}{n}}+\phi\frac{b}{n}\log^{2}(nd)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
, by a union bound.

A.4.
Proof of
Corollary

1.2

We give the details of the proof of the upper bound encoded in (
1.9
). The lower bound will follow from an analogous argument. Fix a rectangle
\mathsf{R}=[a_{l},a_{u}]
in
\mathcal{R}
, where
a_{l}
and
a_{u}
are vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
a_{l}\leq a_{u}
, interpreted component-wise. Define the enlarged rectangle
\mathsf{R}_{t}=[a_{l}-t\bm{1}_{d},a_{u}+t\bm{1}_{d}]
for each
t>0
as well as the normalized Hájek projection

(A.50)

\hat{u}^{(1)}(D_{i})=\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\penalty\ .

Observe that the decomposition

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{b^{2}}}\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})])

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}^{(1)}(D_{i})

(A.51)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{n}{b^{2}}}\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}\left(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)

implies the upper bound

(A.52)

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{\frac{n}{b^{2}}}\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})])\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

(A.53)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}^{(1)}(D_{i})\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}\bigg|

(A.54)

\displaystyle+\bigg|P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\bigg|

(A.55)

\displaystyle+P\left\{\big\|\sqrt{\frac{n}{b^{2}}}\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}\left(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)\big\|_{\infty}>t\right\}\penalty\ .

We bound the three terms appearing in (
A.52
) in succession.

We begin by bounding the normal approximation term (
A.53
) through the application of the following quantitative central limit theorem,
stated as Theorem 2.1 of
Chernozhukov et al., (2022)
.

Lemma A.6

(
Chernozhukov et al.,, 2022
, Theorem 2.1,
)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be a collection
of independent, centered, random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
let
Z
be a centered Gaussian random vector with covariance matrix

(A.56)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}\right].

If there exist absolute constants
c
,
C_{1}
, and
\varphi
such that the bounds

(A.57)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i,j}^{2}\right]\geq c\penalty\ ,\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i,j}^{4}\right]\leq C_{1}\varphi^{2}\penalty\ ,\quad\text{and}\quad\|X_{i,j}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\varphi

hold, then the inequality

(A.58)

\sup_{\mathsf{R}\in\mathcal{R}}\Big|P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\Big|\leq C_{2}\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}

holds for some constant
C_{2}
that depends only on
c
and
C_{1}
.

In particular, observe that

(A.59)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{u}^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)^{2}\right]=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)^{2}\right]}{\sigma^{2}_{b,j}}=1

for each coordinate
j
. Moreover, it holds that

(A.60)

\displaystyle\|\hat{u}^{(1)}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq(\phi/\underline{\sigma}_{b})

for each coordinate
j
by the boundedness condition (
1.8
). Similarly, it holds that

(A.61)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\hat{u}^{(1)}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right)^{4}\right]\leq\left(\phi/\underline{\sigma}_{b}\right)^{2}

for each coordinate
j
, again by boundedness. Consequently, as

(A.62)

\Var(\hat{u}^{(1)}(D_{i}))=\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}\Var\left(Z\right)\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2},

Lemma

A.6
implies that the bound

(A.63)

\bigg|P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}^{(1)}(D_{i})\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}\bigg|\lesssim\left(\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}

holds.

Next, to bound the difference in the Gaussian probabilities (
A.54
), we apply the following anti-concentration inequality, stated in
Chernozhukov et al., (2017)
and often referred to as Nazarov’s inequality.

Lemma A.7

(
Chernozhukov et al.,, 2017
, Theorem 1,
)
.

Let
Z=(Z_{j})_{j=1}^{d}
be a centered Gaussian
random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\mathbb{E}[Z_{j}^{2}]\geq c

for all
j
in
[d]
and some constant
c
. For every
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

and
t>0
, the inequality

P\left\{Z\leq z+t\right\}-P\left\{Z\leq z\right\}\lesssim\frac{t}{c}\sqrt{\log d}

holds.

In particular,
Lemma

A.7
gives

(A.64)

\Big|P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\Big|\lesssim t\sqrt{\log(d)}

for all
t>0
. By choosing

(A.65)

\displaystyle t=C\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(dn)}}\left(\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}

we find that the bound

(A.66)

\displaystyle\Big|P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\Big|\lesssim\left(\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}

holds.

Finally, we bound the term (
A.55
). Observe that it is without loss of generality to assume that

(A.67)

\displaystyle\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}<1

as otherwise the bound (
1.9
) holds vacuously. Now, observe that
Theorem

1.1
implies that

(A.68)

\displaystyle\bigg\|U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\bigg\|_{\infty}\lesssim\phi\frac{b}{n}\log^{2}(dn)

with probability at least
1-C/nd
. Consequently, it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sqrt{\frac{n}{b^{2}}}\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}\left(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)\bigg\|_{\infty}

(A.69)

\displaystyle\lesssim\phi\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}_{b}}\log^{2}(dn)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log(dn)}}\left(\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}\right)^{1/2}

with probability at least
1-C/nd
, where the final inequality follows from (
A.67
).

Hence, by plugging the bounds (
A.63
), (
A.66
), and (
A.69
) into (
A.52
), we find that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{\frac{n}{b^{2}}}\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}(U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})])\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Sigma_{b}^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

(A.70)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\frac{1}{nd}\lesssim\left(\frac{\phi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\sigma}_{b}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}\penalty\ ,

as required.
\blacksquare

A.5.
Proof of
Remark

2.1

Assume that the components of each observation
D_{i}=(D_{i,j})_{j=1}^{d}
are independent and have a standard Gaussian distribution. Consider the kernel

(A.71)

\displaystyle u_{j}(d_{1},\ldots,d_{b})=\frac{\phi}{16b}\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq b}d_{r,j}d_{s,j}

where
d_{k}=(d_{k,j})_{j=1}^{d}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We claim that the bound

(A.72)

\displaystyle\|u_{j}(D_{[b]})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\phi

and the lower bound

(A.73)

\displaystyle P\left\{\bigg\|U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\bigg\|_{\infty}\geq c\frac{\phi b\log(nd)}{n}\right\}\geq\frac{c}{nd}

hold for this choice.

We begin by verifying the bound (
A.72
). Consider the quantity

(A.74)

\displaystyle Q_{j}=\frac{1}{b}\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq b}D_{r,j}D_{s,j}\penalty\ .

Let
A_{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{b\times b}
be the symmetric matrix with zeros on the diagonal and off-diagonal entries equal to
1/(2b)
. The eigenvalues of
A_{b}
are given by

(A.75)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}=\frac{b-1}{2b}\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{2}=\cdots=\lambda_{b}=-\frac{1}{2b}

where we note that

(A.76)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{b}|\lambda_{l}|=\frac{b-1}{b}\leq 1\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{l=1}^{b}\lambda_{l}=0\penalty\ .

Observe that we can write

(A.77)

\displaystyle Q_{j}=D_{j}^{\top}A_{b}D_{j}\penalty\ .

Thus, by the orthogonal invariance of the standard Gaussian law, there exist independent standard Gaussian replicates
G_{1,j},\ldots,G_{b,j}
such that

(A.78)

\displaystyle Q_{j}=\sum_{l=1}^{b}\lambda_{l}G_{l}^{2}=\sum_{l=1}^{b}\lambda_{l}(G_{l}^{2}-1)\penalty\ ,

where the second equality follows from (
A.76
). Consequently, as each replicate
G_{j}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{|G_{j}^{2}-1|}{16}\right)\right]\leq\exp(1/16)\mathbb{E}[\exp(G_{j}^{2}/16)]

(A.79)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(1/16)(1-1/8)^{-1/2}<2\penalty\ ,

we can evaluate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{|Q_{j}|}{16}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\sum_{l=1}^{b}|\lambda_{l}||G_{l}^{2}-1|}{16}\right)\right]

(A.80)

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=1}^{b}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{|G_{l}^{2}-1|}{16}\right)\right]^{|\lambda_{l}|}\leq 2^{\sum_{l=1}^{b}|\lambda_{l}|}\leq 2\penalty\ ,

where the final equality follows from (
A.76
). Hence, the bound

(A.81)

\displaystyle\|Q_{j}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq 16\quad\text{and thereby}\quad\|u_{j}(D_{[b]})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\phi

hold by the definition of the
\psi_{1}
-Orlicz norm.

Next, we verify the bound (
A.73
). First, observe that

(A.82)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{j}(D_{[b]})]

\displaystyle=0

and that

(A.83)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{j}(D_{[b]})\mid D_{1}]=\frac{\phi}{16b}\left(\sum_{l=2}^{b}D_{1,j}\mathbb{E}[D_{l,j}]+\sum_{2\leq r<s\leq b}\mathbb{E}[D_{r,j}D_{s,j}]\right)=0\penalty\ .

Thus, the Hájek projection is given by

(A.84)

\displaystyle u^{(1)}(D_{1})=\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})\mid D_{1}]-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]=0

almost surely. As a consequence, it holds that

(A.85)

\displaystyle\bigg\|U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\bigg\|_{\infty}=\big\|U_{n,b}\big\|_{\infty}

almost surely.

Now, let
U_{n,b,j}(u)
denote the
j
th component of
U_{n,b}(u)
. Observe that, as each unordered pair
\{i,k\}
appears in exactly
{n-2\choose b-2}
subsets
\mathsf{s}
in
\mathcal{S}_{n,b}
, we can evaluate

\displaystyle U_{n,b,j}(u)={n\choose b}^{-1}\sum_{\mathsf{s}\in\mathcal{S}_{n,b}}u_{j}(D_{\mathsf{s}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi}{16b}{n\choose b}^{-1}{n-2\choose b-2}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}D_{i,j}D_{k,j}

(A.86)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(b-1)}{16n(n-1)}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}D_{i,j}D_{k,j}\penalty\ .

By taking an orthonormal change of coordinates in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and identifying

(A.87)

Z_{j}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i,j}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,1)

we can see that there exists
W_{j}\sim\chi^{2}_{n-1}
independent of
Z_{j}
such that

(A.88)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}D^{2}_{i,j}=Z_{j}^{2}+W_{j}\penalty\ .

Thus, we can write

\displaystyle U_{n,b,j}(u)=\frac{\phi(b-1)}{16n(n-1)}\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}D_{i,j}D_{k,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(b-1)}{32n(n-1)}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i,j}\right)^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}D_{i,j}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(b-1)}{32n(n-1)}\left(nZ_{j}^{2}-(Z_{j}^{2}+W_{j})\right)

(A.89)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(b-1)}{32n(n-1)}\left((n-1)Z_{j}^{2}-W_{j}\right)\penalty\ ,

almost surely. Thus, the representations (
A.85
) and (
A.89
) implies that, on the event

(A.90)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=\left\{Z_{1}^{2}\geq\log(end)+2,W_{1}\leq 2(n-1)\right\}\penalty\ ,

it holds that

(A.91)

\displaystyle\bigg\|U_{n,b}(u)-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\bigg\|_{\infty}=\big\|U_{n,b}(u)\big\|_{\infty}\geq\phi\frac{b-1}{32n}\log(end)\penalty\ .

Now, observe that

(A.92)

\displaystyle P\{Z_{j}\geq x\}\geq cx^{-1}\exp(-x^{2}/2)\quad\text{and}\quad P\{W_{j}\leq 2(n-1)\}\geq 1/2\penalty\ ,

where the first inequality is a standard lower bound for Gaussian random variables and
W_{j}
follows from Markov’s inequality. Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle P\{\mathcal{A}\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}P\{Z_{1}^{2}\geq\log(end)+2\}

(A.93)

\displaystyle\gtrsim(\log(end)+2)^{-1/2}(end)^{-1/2}\gtrsim(nd)^{-1}\penalty\ .

The claim is verified by combining (
A.91
) and (
A.93
).
\blacksquare

Appendix B
An Abstract Bound on Coverage Error

In this Appendix, we give an abstract bound on the accuracy of the nominal coverage probability for the confidence region introduced in
Definition

3.1
. We make no use of the kernel structure expressed in (
3.2
). The proof is given in
Section

B.1
. We require several mild smoothness restrictions on the moment function
M(\cdot;\theta,g)
. In contrast to the set of assumptions specified in
Section

3.3
, we do not require moment linearity. Instead, we impose the following generalization of Part (iii) of
Assumption

3.3
.

Assumption B.1

(Moment Restrictions)
.

The moment function
M(x;\theta_{0},g_{0})
is twice continuously differentiable in its second argument. Let

(B.1)

M^{(1)}(x;\theta,g)=\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}M(x;\theta^{\prime},g)|_{\theta^{\prime}=\theta}\quad\text{and}\quad H(x;\theta,g)=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\theta^{\prime}}M(x;\theta^{\prime},g)|_{\theta^{\prime}=\theta}

denote the Jacobian and Hessian of
M(\cdot;\theta,g)
in
\theta
, respectively. The Jacobian
M^{(1)}(x;\theta,g)
is uniformly Lipschitz in its second argument and bounded from below in the sense that

(B.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathbf{P}}\sup_{j\in[d]}\big|M^{(1)}(x^{(j)};\theta,g)-M^{(1)}(x^{(j)};\theta,g_{0})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g-g_{0}\|_{2,\infty}\quad\text{and}

(B.3)

\displaystyle\inf_{P\in\mathbf{P}}\inf_{j\in[d]}\big|M^{(1)}(x^{(j)};\theta,g)\big|

\displaystyle\geq c

for each
g
and
\theta
and some positive constant
c
. The Hessian
H(x;\theta,g)
is uniformly bounded as
x
,
\theta
, and
g
vary over their respective domains.

Moreover, we impose an analogous restriction on the centered empirical moment

(B.4)

\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta,g)=M_{n}(x;\theta,g,\mathbf{D}_{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(x;\theta,g,\mathbf{D}_{n})\right]\penalty\ ,

where we have made the dependence on
\mathbf{D}_{n}
implicit to ease notation.

Assumption B.2

(Empirical Smoothness)
.

The centered empirical moment (
B.4
) is twice continuously differentiable in its second argument. Let

(B.5)

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{n}(x;\theta,g)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\theta^{\prime}}\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta^{\prime},g_{0})|_{\theta^{\prime}=\theta}

denote the Hessian of
\overline{M}_{n}(\cdot;\theta,g)
in
\theta
, respectively. The Hessian
\overline{H}_{n}(x;\theta,g)
is uniformly bounded almost surely as
x
,
\theta
, and
g
vary over their respective domains.

Next, we impose a set of high-level restrictions on the structure of the empirical conditional moment (
3.2
). At times we refer to the normalized statistic

(B.6)

W_{n}(x)=-(M^{(1)}(x;g_{0}))^{-1}\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0},g_{0})\penalty\ .

First, we impose a condition that ensures that (
B.6
) is approximately linear. Fix a generic sequence
\overline{q}_{n,d}
.

Assumption B.3

(Approximate Linearity)
.

There exists a function
\overline{u}(\cdot,\cdot)
, a constant
\varphi\geq 1
, and a real-valued sequence
\delta_{n,u}
such that

(B.7)

\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right]=0\penalty\ ,\quad\|\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\varphi\penalty\ ,

and

(B.8)

\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{u}^{4}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right]\leq\Var(\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i}))\varphi^{2}

hold for all
j
in
[d]
and
P
in
\mathbf{P}
. Define the moment
\lambda^{2}_{j}=\Var(\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i}))
and set
\underline{\lambda}^{2}=\min_{j\in[d]}\lambda^{2}_{j}
. The higher-order moment inequality

(B.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left(W_{n}(x^{(j)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]^{1/q}\leq q^{2}\delta_{n,u}\penalty\ .

holds for all
j
in
[d]
,
2\vee 2\log(nd)\leq q\leq\overline{q}_{n,d}
, and uniformly over
P
in
\mathbf{P}
,

Remark B.1
.

Compare
Assumption

B.3
with the bound

(B.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|U_{n,b}(u_{j})-\mathbb{E}[u_{j}(D_{[b]})]-\frac{b}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u_{j}^{(1)}(D_{i})|^{2\log(nd)}\right]^{1/(2\log(dn))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\phi\frac{b}{n}\log^{2}(dn)

obtained from
Lemma

2.2
. The bound (
B.10
) implies
Theorem

1.1
by Markov’s inequality.
\blacksquare

Second, we impose several restrictions relating to the estimators
\hat{\theta}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})
and
\hat{g}_{n}
. A closely related collection of conditions is stated in
Chernozhukov et al., (2018)
.

Assumption B.4

(Bias, Consistency, and Stochastic Equicontinuity)
.

Recall the definition of the object
\underline{\lambda}^{2}
introduced in
Assumption

B.3
. Let

(B.11)

\displaystyle\overline{M}^{(1)}_{n}(x;\theta,g)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta^{\prime},g)|_{\theta^{\prime}=\theta}

denote the Jacobian of the centered empirical moment
\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta,g)
.

(i)
Define the quantity

(B.12)

\mathsf{Bias}_{n}(x;\theta,g)=M(x;\theta,g)-\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(x;\theta,g,\bm{D}_{n})\right]\penalty\ .

There exists a sequence
\delta_{n,B}
such that

(B.13)

\sup_{P\in\mathbf{P}}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\|\mathsf{Bias}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\theta(\bm{x}^{(d)}),g)\|_{\infty}\lesssim(1+\|\theta(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty})\delta_{n,B}

uniformly over any vector
\theta(\bm{x}^{(d)})=\{\theta(x^{(j)})\}_{j=1}^{d}
.

(ii)
There exist sequences
\delta_{n,m}
,
\delta_{n,g}
, and
\delta_{n,\theta}
such that, uniformly over
P
in
\mathbf{P}
,

(B.14)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\mathbb{E}\left[\big|\overline{M}^{(1)}_{n}(x^{(j)};\theta_{0}(x^{(j)}),g_{0})\big|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq q\delta_{n,m}\penalty\ ,

(B.15)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{2,\infty}^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq q\delta_{n,g}\penalty\ ,\quad\text{and}

(B.16)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\mathbb{E}\left[\big|\hat{\theta}_{n}(x^{(j)})-\theta_{0}(x^{(j)})\big|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq q\delta_{n,\theta}

holds for all
j
in
[d]
and
2\vee 2\log(nd)\leq q\leq\overline{q}_{n,d}
.

(iii)
There exist sequences
\delta_{n,S}
and
\delta_{n,J}
such that, uniformly over
P
in
\mathbf{P}
,

(B.17)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\big|\overline{M}_{n}(x^{(j)};\theta_{0}(x^{(j)}),\hat{g}_{n})-\overline{M}_{n}(x^{(j)};\theta_{0}(x^{(j)}),g_{0})\big|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq q\delta_{n,S}

and

(B.18)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\big|\overline{M}^{(1)}_{n}(x^{(j)};\theta_{0}(x^{(j)}),\hat{g}_{n})-\overline{M}^{(1)}_{n}(x^{(j)};\theta_{0}(x^{(j)}),g_{0})\big|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq q\delta_{n,J}\penalty\ ,

holds for all
j
in
[d]
and
2\vee 2\log(nd)\leq q\leq\overline{q}_{n,d}
.

The following theorem gives a non-asymptotic bound on the error in the nominal coverage probability of the confidence regions introduced in
Definition

3.1
.

Theorem B.1
.

Collect the error sequences

\displaystyle\delta_{n}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}_{n,g}+\delta^{2}_{n,\theta}+\delta^{2}_{n,m}+\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,S}+\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}\delta_{n,\theta}\left(\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,J}\right)+\delta_{n,u}

and assume that
\delta_{\varepsilon n}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\delta_{n}
for any
0<\varepsilon<1
. Suppose that the Neyman orthogonal moment function
M(x;\theta_{0},g_{0})
satisfies
Assumption

B.1
and Part (i) of
Assumption

3.3
and that the centered empirical moment function
\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0},g_{0})
satisfies
Assumption

B.2
. If
Assumption

B.3
and
Assumption

B.4
hold, then the confidence region defined in
Definition

3.1
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{P\in\mathbf{P}}\big|P\left\{\theta_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\hat{\mathcal{C}}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\right\}-(1-\alpha)\big|

(B.19)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}\left(dn\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ .

B.1.
Proof of
Theorem

B.1

Throughout, without loss, we will assume that

(B.20)

\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\leq c\penalty\ ,

as otherwise the desired bound (
B.19
) is vacuous.

It will suffice to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}<z\right\}-P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}<z\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}\big|

(B.21)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}

with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
. To see this, let
\mathsf{cv}(\gamma)
denote the
1-\gamma
quantile of
\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}
for each
\gamma
in
(0,1)
and fix

(B.22)

\beta_{n,d}=\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ .

Observe that (
B.21
) implies that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}<\mathsf{cv}(\alpha-\beta_{n,d})\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}

(B.23)

\displaystyle\geq P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}<\mathsf{cv}(\alpha-\beta_{n,d})\right\}-\beta_{n,d}\geq 1-\alpha

and

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}<\mathsf{cv}(\alpha+\beta_{n,d})\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}

(B.24)

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}<\mathsf{cv}(\alpha+\beta_{n,d})\right\}+\beta_{n,d}\leq 1-\alpha

each with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
. Thus, recalling that
\widehat{\mathsf{cv}}(\alpha)
denotes the
1-\alpha
quantile of
\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}
conditioned on the data
\mathbf{D}_{n}
, (
B.23
) and (
B.24
) imply that

\displaystyle P\left\{\mathsf{cv}(\alpha+\beta_{n,d})<\hat{\mathsf{cv}}(\alpha)<\mathsf{cv}(\alpha-\beta_{n,d})\right\}

(B.25)

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1-\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/2}-\frac{1}{nd}\geq 1-\beta_{n,d}\penalty\ ,

where the second inequality follows from the fact that the normalization (
B.20
) implies that

(B.26)

\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}\penalty\ .

Consequently, as we can write

(B.27)

\displaystyle P\left\{\bm{\theta}_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\hat{\mathcal{C}}(\bm{x}^{(d)},\mathbf{D}_{n})\right\}=P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq\hat{\mathsf{cv}}(\alpha)\right\}

by definition, the inequality (
B.25
) implies that

\displaystyle P\left\{\bm{\theta}_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\hat{\mathcal{C}}(\bm{x}^{(d)},\mathbf{D}_{n})\right\}

(B.28)

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{cv}(\alpha-\beta_{n,d})\right\}+\beta_{n,d}\lesssim 1-\alpha+\beta_{n,d}

and

\displaystyle P\left\{\bm{\theta}_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\hat{\mathcal{C}}(\bm{x}^{(d)},\mathbf{D}_{n})\right\}

(B.29)

\displaystyle\geq P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq\mathsf{cv}(\alpha+\beta_{n,d})\right\}-\beta_{n,d}\gtrsim 1-\alpha-\beta_{n,d}

respectively, as required.

Hence, the remainder of the proof is devoted to establishing the bound (
B.21
). To do this, we require some additional notation. Let
\mathcal{R}
denote the set of hyper-rectangles in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
Z
denote a centered Gaussian random vector with covariance matrix
\Var(\overline{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i}))
. Let
\Lambda
be the diagonal matrix with components
\lambda_{j}^{2}=\Var(\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i}))
. To verify (
B.21
) it will suffice to establish that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathsf{R}\in\mathcal{R}}\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\big|

(B.30)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}

and that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathsf{R}\in\mathcal{R}}\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\big|

(B.31)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-Cn^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)-(nd)^{-1}
. That is, the probability bound (
B.21
) follows from (
B.30
) and (
B.31
) by considering hyper-rectangles of the form
\mathsf{R}=[-\infty\bm{1}_{d},z\bm{1}_{d}]
and applying the triangle inequality.

We provide the details for the proofs of the upper bounds encoded in the absolute inequalities (
B.30
) and (
B.31
), respectively. Analogous lower bounds will follow from the same argument. In particular, fix a rectangle
\mathsf{R}=[a_{l},a_{u}]
in
\mathcal{R}
,
where
a_{l}
and
a_{u}
are vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
a_{l}\leq a_{u}
, interpreted component-wise, and define the enlarged rectangle
\mathsf{R}_{t}=[a_{l}-t\bm{1}_{d},a_{u}+t\bm{1}_{d}]
for each
t>0
. We obtain upper bounds

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

(B.32)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}\big|

(B.33)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\big|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\big|

(B.34)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|(\Lambda^{-1/2}-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2})R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\geq t\right\},

and similarly

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

(B.35)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}|

(B.36)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}|

(B.37)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|(\Lambda^{-1/2}-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2})R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\geq t\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}\penalty\ ,

respectively. It thereby remains to obtain suitable bounds for each term, (
B.32
) through (
B.37
).

To bound the Gaussian approximation errors (
B.32
)
and (
B.35
), we apply the following
Theorem, which establishes a generic quantitative central limit for the statistic
R_{n}
in addition to a generic quantitative conditional central limit theorem for the half-sample bootstrap.

Theorem B.2
.

Suppose that the moment function
M(x;\theta_{0},g_{0})
satisfies
Assumption

B.1
and Part (i) of
Assumption

3.3
and that
B.3
and
B.4
hold.

(i)
The inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{R\in\mathcal{R}}\sup_{P\in\mathbf{P}}\Big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\Big|

(B.38)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varphi^{1/2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}\left(\frac{\log^{5}(dn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}

holds.

(ii)
If the bootstrap root is constructed with the Half-Sample bootstrap, then the inequality

\displaystyle\sup_{R\in\mathcal{R}}\sup_{P\in\mathbf{P}}\Big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\Big|

(B.39)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varphi^{1/2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}\left(\frac{\log^{5}\left(dn\right)}{n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

holds with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
.

In particular,
Theorem

B.2
implies that

\displaystyle\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}\big|

\displaystyle=\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\Lambda^{1/2}\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\Lambda^{1/2}\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}\big|

(B.40)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\frac{\varphi^{1/2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}\left(\frac{\log^{5}(dn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}

and analogously

\displaystyle|P\left\{\sqrt{n}\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}|

\displaystyle=\big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\Lambda^{1/2}\mathsf{R}_{t}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\Lambda^{1/2}\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}\big|

(B.41)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\frac{\varphi^{1/2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}\left(\frac{\log^{5}(dn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
, as
\Lambda^{1/2}\mathsf{R}_{t}
is a hyper-rectangle.

To bound the differences in the Gaussian probabilities (
B.33
)
and (
B.36
), we apply
Lemma

A.7
. In particular, we have that

(B.42)

|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}|\lesssim t\sqrt{\log d}

for all
t>0
.

Finally, we bound the terms (
B.34
) and (
B.37
) resulting from variance estimation. Observe that

\displaystyle\|(\Lambda^{-1/2}-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2})R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|(I-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}\Lambda^{1/2})\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

(B.43)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\in[d]}|\lambda_{j}/\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}-1|\cdot\|\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

and analogously

(B.44)

\displaystyle\|(\Lambda^{-1/2}-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2})R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{j\in[d]}|\lambda_{j}/\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}-1|\cdot\|\Lambda^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\penalty\ .

We give probability bounds for the terms on the right-hand sides of (
B.43
) and (
B.44
). Observe that the Borell-TIS inequality
(
Adler and Taylor,, 2009
, e.g., Theorem 2.1.1 of)
implies that

P\left\{\|\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\|_{\infty}\geq C\sqrt{\log{dn}}\right\}\leq n^{-1}\penalty\ .

Thus,
Theorem

B.2
implies that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\geq C\sqrt{\log{dn}}\right\}

(B.45)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}

and that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\Lambda^{-1/2}R^{*}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\geq C\sqrt{\log{dn}}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}

(B.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
. In turn, to bound the discrepancy between the bootstrap variance estimate
\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}
and
\lambda_{j}
, we apply the following Lemma.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose that the conditions of
Theorem

B.1
hold. If the bootstrap root is constructed with the Half-Sample bootstrap, then

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\bigg|\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}}{\lambda_{j}}-1\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}

(B.47)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
.

To apply
Lemma

B.1
, observe that

(B.48)

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}}-1\bigg|=\frac{|\lambda_{j}/\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}-1|}{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}/\lambda_{j}}\penalty\ .

Thus, the event that

(B.49)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\bigg|\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}}{\lambda_{j}}-1\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{implies}\quad\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}}{\lambda_{j}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\penalty\ .

Hence, on this event, we find that

(B.50)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\bigg|\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}}-1\bigg|\leq 2\sup_{j\in[d]}\bigg|\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}}{\lambda_{j}}-1\bigg|\penalty\ .

Observe now that the normalization (
B.20
) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}

(B.51)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)<c

and so the condition (
B.49
) is satisfied by the event characterized in
Lemma

B.1
. Thus, by combining the bounds (
B.43
), (
B.45
), and (
B.50
) with
Lemma

B.1
, we find that

\displaystyle P\bigg\{\sqrt{n}\|(\Lambda^{-1/2}-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2})R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\gtrsim\bigg(\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}\bigg)\sqrt{\log{dn}}\bigg\}

(B.52)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ .

Similarly, by combining the bounds (
B.44
), (
B.46
), and (
B.50
) with Lemma (
B.1
), we find that

\displaystyle P\bigg\{\sqrt{n}\|(\Lambda^{-1/2}-\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2})R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\gtrsim\left(\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\sqrt{\log{dn}}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\bigg\}

(B.53)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
.

To put the pieces together, recall the upper bounds (
B.32
) and (
B.35
). By setting

(B.54)

\displaystyle t=\left(\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\sqrt{\log{dn}}\penalty\ ,

the bounds (
B.40
), (
B.42
), and (
B.52
) imply that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\left(\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\log(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}

(B.55)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ .

Indeed, the second inequality follows from the normalization (
B.20
). In particular,

(B.56)

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}\log(dn)+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\log(dn)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}\penalty\ ,

while

(B.57)

\displaystyle\log^{3}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{5}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)\penalty\ .

Similarly, the bounds (
B.41
), (
B.42
), and (
B.53
) imply that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}\hat{\Lambda}_{n}^{-1/2}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\left(\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\log(dn)

(B.58)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{3}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ ,

with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
. Hence, the bounds (
B.55
) and (
B.58
) verify the bounds encoded in (
B.30
) and (
B.31
), as required.

B.2.
Proof of
Theorem

B.2

B.2.1.
Part (i)

Throughout, we take
\theta_{0}(x)=0
for all
x
without loss of generality. We begin by giving a high-probability bound on the difference

(B.59)

\|R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})-W_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

which will be needed at a later point in the proof. To this end, let
x
be any component of the vector
\bm{x}^{(d)}
. By a Taylor expansion about
\theta_{0}(x)
, we have that

\displaystyle M(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-M(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

(B.60)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))^{2}H(x;\tilde{\theta}_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

for some
\tilde{\theta}_{0}(x)
between
\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)
and
\theta_{0}(x)
. Moreover, we can write

\displaystyle(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})

(B.61)

\displaystyle+(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))\left(M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})\right)

and

\displaystyle M(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-M(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=(M(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-M_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n},\mathbf{D}_{n}))

\displaystyle\quad+(M(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})-M(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n}))

\displaystyle=\left(M(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n},\mathbf{D}_{n})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n},\mathbf{D}_{n})\right]-M_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n},\mathbf{D}_{n})\right)

(B.62)

\displaystyle\quad+(M(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})-M(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n}))\penalty\ .

Thus, by the identity

\displaystyle\overline{M}_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=\overline{M}_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

(B.63)

\displaystyle+\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})+\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})\penalty\ ,

the equalities (
B.60
), (
B.61
), and (
B.62
) imply that

\displaystyle M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))

\displaystyle=M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))

\displaystyle-\left(M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0},g_{0})\right)(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))

(B.64)

\displaystyle=-\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})

(B.65)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathsf{Bias}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})+\mathsf{Nuis}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

(B.66)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathsf{Stoch}^{(1)}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})+\mathsf{Stoch}^{(2)}(x;\hat{g}_{n})

(B.67)

\displaystyle\quad-(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))^{2}H(x;\tilde{\theta}_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

(B.68)

\displaystyle\quad-(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))\left(M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-M^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})\right)\penalty\

where

(B.69)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Bias}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=M(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[M_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})\right]\penalty\ ,

(B.70)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Nuis}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=M(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})-M(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})\penalty\ ,

(B.71)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Stoch}^{(1)}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=\overline{M}_{n}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})\penalty\ ,\quad\text{and}

(B.72)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Stoch}^{(2)}(x;\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})-\overline{M}_{n}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})\penalty\ ,

respectively.

We now give moment bounds for the terms (
B.65
), (
B.66
), (
B.67
), and (
B.68
). Fix any
q\geq 2\vee 2\log(nd)
. We repeatedly use the fact that, for any random vector
Z(\bm{x}^{(d)})=(Z(x^{(j)}))_{j=1}^{d}
,

(B.73)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|Z(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}^{q}\right]^{1/q}\leq d^{1/q}\max_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z(x^{(j)})|^{q}\right]^{1/q}\lesssim\max_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z(x^{(j)})|^{q}\right]^{1/q}\penalty\ ,

where the final inequality follows from the lower bound on
q
. To handle (
B.65
), observe that Parts (i) and (ii) of
Assumption

B.4
imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left\|\mathsf{Bias}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\hat{\theta}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)}),\hat{g}_{n})\right\|_{\infty}\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{n,B}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\theta}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}^{q}\right]^{1/q}\right)

(B.74)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{n,B}\left(1+q\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}\delta_{n,\theta}\right)\lesssim q^{2}\left(\delta_{n,B}+\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}\delta_{n,\theta}\delta_{n,B}\right)\penalty\ .

Moreover, a Taylor expansion, Neyman orthogonality, second-order smoothness, i.e.,
Assumption

B.1
, and
Assumption

B.4
, Part (ii), give that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left\|\mathsf{Nuis}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\theta_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)}),\hat{g}_{n})\right\|_{\infty}\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

(B.75)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\|\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\left(\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\right)^{1/4}\|\hat{g}_{n}-g_{0}\|_{2,\infty}\right|^{2q}\right]^{1/q}\lesssim q^{2}\delta_{n,g}^{2}\penalty\ .

Next, we handle the term (
B.66
). By
Assumption

B.2
, a Taylor expansion gives

\displaystyle\mathsf{Stoch}^{(1)}(x;\hat{\theta}_{n}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

\displaystyle=(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))\overline{M}_{n}^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

(B.76)

\displaystyle\quad+(\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))^{2}\overline{H}_{n}(x;\tilde{\theta}_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})

for some, potentially different,
\tilde{\theta}_{0}(x)
between
\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)
and
\theta_{0}(x)
. To bound this term, observe that

\displaystyle|\overline{M}_{n}^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})|

\displaystyle\leq|\overline{M}_{n}^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})|

(B.77)

\displaystyle\quad+|\overline{M}_{n}^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),\hat{g}_{n})-\overline{M}_{n}^{(1)}(x;\theta_{0}(x),g_{0})|\penalty\ .

Hence,
Assumption

B.2
and
Assumption

B.4
, Parts (ii) and (iii), together with Holder’s inequality, imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left\|\mathsf{Stoch}^{(1)}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\hat{\theta}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)}),\hat{g}_{n})\right\|_{\infty}\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

(B.78)

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{2}\delta_{n,\theta}\delta_{n,m}+q^{2}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}\delta_{n,\theta}\delta_{n,J}+q^{2}\delta_{n,\theta}^{2}\penalty\ .

Moreover,
Assumption

B.4
, Part (iii), gives that

(B.79)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left\|\mathsf{Stoch}^{(2)}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\hat{g}_{n})\right\|_{\infty}\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}\lesssim q\delta_{n,S}\lesssim q^{2}\delta_{n,S}\penalty\ .

Finally, we handle the terms (
B.67
) and (
B.68
).
Assumption

B.4
, Part (ii), and
Assumption

B.1
imply that

(B.80)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left\|(\hat{\theta}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})-\theta_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)}))^{2}H(\bm{x}^{(d)};\tilde{\theta}_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)}),\hat{g}_{n})\right\|_{\infty}\right|^{q}\bigg]^{1/q}\lesssim q^{2}\delta_{n,\theta}^{2}\penalty\ ,

and, by the Lipschitz property of
M^{(1)}(\cdot;\theta,g)
in
g
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\bigg\|(\hat{\theta}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})-\theta_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left(M^{(1)}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\theta_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)}),\hat{g}_{n})-M^{(1)}(\bm{x}^{(d)};\theta_{0}(\bm{x}^{(d)}),g_{0})\right)\bigg\|_{\infty}\bigg|^{q}\bigg]^{1/q}

(B.81)

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{2}\delta_{n,\theta}\delta_{n,g}\penalty\ .

Together, the decomposition (
B.64
) and the lower-boundedness of the Jacobian
M^{(1)}(\cdot;\theta,g)
imply that

(B.82)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\left\|R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})-W_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\right\|_{\infty}\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{2}\bigg(\delta_{n,g}^{2}+\delta_{n,\theta}^{2}+\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,S}+\delta_{n,\theta}\left(\delta_{n,m}+\delta_{n,g}+\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}\left(\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,J}\right)\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{2}\bigg(\delta_{n,g}^{2}+\delta_{n,\theta}^{2}+\delta_{n,m}^{2}+\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,S}+\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}\delta_{n,\theta}\left(\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,J}\right)\bigg)\penalty\ ,

where the final inequality uses
ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
, applied to the products

\delta_{n,\theta}\delta_{n,m}
and
\delta_{n,\theta}\delta_{n,g}
.

With this in place, we turn to the proof of the normal approximation to
R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})
on hyper-rectangles. Fix a rectangle
\mathsf{R}=[a_{l},a_{u}]
in
\mathcal{R}
. For the sake of exposition, we give the details of the proof of the upper bound

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

(B.83)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ .

The matching lower bound will follow from a very similar argument. Consider the decomposition

(B.84)

\displaystyle R_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=\left(W_{n}(x)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})+\Delta_{n}(x),\quad\text{where}

(B.85)

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=R_{n}(x)-W_{n}(x)\penalty\ ,

and we recall that the function
\overline{u}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in
Assumption

B.3
. Define the normalized functions

\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})=\Lambda^{-1/2}\overline{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{W}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})=\Lambda^{-1/2}W_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})

and the analogously normalized rectangle
\tilde{\mathsf{R}}=[\Lambda^{-1/2}a_{l},\Lambda^{-1/2}a_{u}]
and the enlarged rectangle
\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}=[\Lambda^{-1/2}a_{l}-\mathbf{1}_{d}t,\Lambda^{-1/2}a_{u}+\mathbf{1}_{d}t]
. Observe that the decomposition (
B.84
) yields the upper bound

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

\displaystyle=P\left\{\sqrt{n}\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\right\}

(B.86)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\Big|P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}\Big|

(B.87)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Big|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\right\}\Big|

(B.88)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\hat{W}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{1}{2}t\right\}

(B.89)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+P\left\{\sqrt{n}\|\Lambda^{-1/2}\Delta_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{1}{2}t\right\}

for each
t>0
. We proceed by providing appropriate bounds for the terms (
B.86
) through (
B.89
).

We begin by bounding the normal approximation term through an application of
Lemma

A.6
. In particular, observe that

(B.90)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right]=1

by definition and that

(B.91)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{u}^{4}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right]\leq\left(\varphi/\underline{\lambda}\right)^{2}

by
Assumption

B.3
. Similarly we have that

(B.92)

\|\hat{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq(\varphi/\underline{\lambda})

by
Assumption

B.3
. Consequently, as

(B.93)

\Var(\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i}))=\Lambda^{-1/2}\Var\left(Z\right)\Lambda^{-1/2},

by definition, Lemma
A.6
implies that the bound

(B.94)

\Big|P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\mathbf{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\mathsf{\tilde{R}}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{\tilde{R}}_{t}\right\}\Big|\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}\penalty\ .

holds.

In turn, to bound the terms (
B.87
), we have that

(B.95)

\Big|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\right\}\Big|\lesssim t\sqrt{\log d}\penalty\ .

by
Lemma

A.7
. To bound the terms (
B.88
), taking
q=2\log(nd)
,
Assumption

B.3
implies that

(B.96)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\hat{W}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta_{n,u}\log^{2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
, by applying Markov’s inequality and a union bound. Likewise, the bound (
B.82
) implies that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\Lambda^{-1/2}\Delta_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}

(B.97)

\displaystyle\lesssim(\delta_{n,g}^{2}+\delta_{n,\theta}^{2}+\delta_{n,m}^{2}+\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,S}+\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}n^{-1/4}\delta_{n,\theta}(\delta_{n,B}+\delta_{n,J}))\log^{2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
, by applying Markov’s inequality and a union bound.

Thus, by choosing
t=C\delta_{n}\log^{2}(dn)
, the sum of term (
B.88
) and term (
B.89
) is
upper bounded by
C/nd
. Hence, by plugging this choice of
t
into (
B.95
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\Big|P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\Big|

(B.98)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)+\frac{1}{nd}\penalty\ ,

as required.

B.2.2.
Part (ii)

The result follows from an argument whose structure is similar to the proof of
Theorem

B.2
, Part (i). Again, we take
\theta_{0}(x)=0
for all
x
, without loss of generality. We are interested in studying the discrepancy

\displaystyle R_{n}^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\hat{\theta}_{\mathsf{h}}(x)-\hat{\theta}_{n}(x)=(\hat{\theta}_{\mathsf{h}}(x)-\theta_{0}(x))-R_{n}(x).

In terms of the decomposition (
B.84
), we can write

(B.99)

\displaystyle R_{n}(x)=\left(W_{n}(x)-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})+\Delta_{n}(x)\penalty\ ,

where
\Delta_{n}(x)
is defined in (
B.85
). On the other hand, as
\hat{\theta}_{\mathsf{h}}(x)
is constructed with a random half-sample
\mathsf{h}
of the data
\mathbf{D}_{n}
,
we have that

(B.100)

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{\mathsf{h}}(x)-\theta_{0}(x)=\left(\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})-W_{\mathsf{h}}(x)\right)+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})+\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x)\penalty\ ,

where
U_{\mathsf{h}}(x)
and
\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x)
are constructed with the half-sample
\mathsf{h}
.

The proof of
Theorem

B.2
, Part (i), worked by giving a high probability bound for the first and third term in (
B.99
) and showing that the second term satisfies a central limit theorem. Here, as we are interested in giving a bound conditioned on the data
\mathbf{D}_{n}
, we show that the difference between the second terms in (
B.99
) and (
B.100
) satisfies a central limit theorem on the event that the first and third terms in (
B.99
) and (
B.100
) satisfy a specified bound, which we show holds with high probability. To this end, let
\hat{W}_{\mathsf{h}}(\bm{x}^{(d)})
is defined analogously to
\hat{W}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})
and let
\mathcal{F}_{n}(t)
and
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{h}}(t)
denote the events that

(B.101)

\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\|\Delta_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq t/4\quad\text{and}\quad\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}\|\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq t/4\penalty\ ,

respectively. Similarly, let
\mathcal{H}_{n}(t)
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{h}}(t)
denote the events that

(B.102)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})-\hat{W}_{n}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq t/4\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\|\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})-\hat{W}_{\mathsf{h}}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\|_{\infty}\leq t/4

respectively. Define the event
\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)=\mathcal{F}_{n}(t)\cap\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{h}}(t)\cap\mathcal{H}_{n}(t)\cap\mathcal{H}_{\mathsf{h}}(t)
.

Fix a hyper-rectangle
\mathsf{R}
in
\mathcal{R}
. As before, we prove only the requisite upper bound. Recall the definitions of the transformed rectangle
\tilde{\mathsf{R}}
and the enlarged transformed rectangle
\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}
. On the event
\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)
, we have

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

\displaystyle=P\left\{\sqrt{n}\Lambda^{-1/2}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}

(B.103)

\displaystyle+|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}|

for each
t>0
. As the data
D_{\mathsf{h}}
are drawn independently and identically with distribution
P
in
\mathbf{P}
and we have assumed that
\delta_{n/2}\lesssim\delta_{n}
, by setting
t=C\delta_{n}\log^{2}(dn)
, bounds (
B.97
) and (
B.96
) imply that the event
\mathcal{E}_{n}(t)
occurs with probability greater than
1-C/nd
. Thus,
Lemma

A.7
implies that

(B.104)

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}

(B.105)

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\tilde{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\right\}

\displaystyle+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
. Hence, it suffices to bound the term (
B.105
).

To this end, we apply a coupling argument introduced in
Yadlowsky et al., (2023)
, which is similar to a Poissonization technique studied in
Præstgaard and Wellner, (1993)
(see also Section 3.6.2 of
Van der Vaart and Wellner, (1996)
). In particular, let
V_{i}
be a random variable taking the value
1
when
i

is an element of the subset
\mathsf{h}
and taking the value
-1
otherwise. Observe that

(B.106)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\penalty\ .

Let
\tilde{V}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{V}_{n}
denote a collection of random
variables valued on
\{-1,1\}
. We define their joint distribution
as follows. Let
Q_{n}
denote a random variable with distribution

\mathsf{Bin}\left(n,1/2\right)
. If
Q_{n}\geq n/2
, then choose
Q_{n}-n/2

indices
i
in
[n]
with
V_{i}=-1
and set
\tilde{V}_{i}=1
.
If
Q_{n}<n/2
, then choose
n/2-Q_{n}
indices with
V_{i}=1

and set
\tilde{V}_{i}=-1
. Set
\tilde{V}_{i}=V_{i}
for all other
units. Observe that the collection
\tilde{V}_{i}
are independent
and identically distributed Rademacher random variables. With this in place,
we obtain the decomposition

(B.107)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{V}_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(V_{i}-\tilde{V}_{i}\right)\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\penalty\ .

Let
\mathcal{V}(t^{\prime})
denote the event that

(B.108)

\sqrt{n}\Big\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(V_{i}-\tilde{V}_{i}\right)\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}<t^{\prime}\penalty\ .

Fix any rectangle
\mathsf{R}^{\prime}=[a^{\prime}_{l},a^{\prime}_{u}]
and define the enlarged rectangle
\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}=[a^{\prime}_{l}-t^{\prime}\mathbf{1}_{d},a^{\prime}_{u}+t^{\prime}\mathbf{1}_{d}]
.
On the event
\mathcal{V}(t^{\prime})
, the decomposition (
B.107
) implies that

\displaystyle P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\right\}

(B.109)

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{V}_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\right\}

(B.110)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\bigg|P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\right\}\bigg|

for all
t>0
.
Lemma

A.7
implies that (
B.110
) is less than
t^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(d)}
. To handle the term (
B.109
), we apply the following quantitative central limit theorem, stated as Lemma 4.6 in
Chernozhukov et al., (2022)
.

Lemma B.2

(
Chernozhukov et al.,, 2022
, Lemma 4.6,
)
.

Consider the setting and assumptions
of
Lemma

A.6
. Let
\bm{X}_{n}=(X_{1},\ldots,X_{n})

collect the observed data and let
\tilde{V}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{V}_{n}
be a collection
of independent Rademacher random variables. We have that

(B.111)

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathsf{R}\in\mathcal{R}}\Big|P\left\{n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\in\mathsf{R}\right\}-P\left\{n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{V}_{i}X_{i}\in\mathsf{R}\mid\bm{X}_{n}\right\}\Big|

(B.112)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\leq C_{3}\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{n}\right)^{1/4}.

with probability greater than

(B.113)

1-C\frac{\varphi\log^{3/2}(dn)}{n^{1/2}}

for some constant
C_{3}
that depends only on the constants
C_{2}
and
c
defined in the statement of
Lemma

A.6
.

Thus, on the event
\mathcal{V}(t^{\prime})
,
Lemma

A.6
and
Lemma

B.2
imply that

\displaystyle P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)};D_{i})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\right\}\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+t^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(d)}\penalty\ ,

with probability greater than
1-Cn^{-1/2}\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\varphi\log^{3/2}(dn)
.

Hence, it suffices to give a high probability bound on
\mathcal{V}(t^{\prime})
. To this end, observe that

(B.114)

\displaystyle G_{n}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(V_{i}-\tilde{V}_{i}\right)\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)};D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}

is equidistributed with

(B.115)

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{|Q_{n}-n/2|}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)};D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}\penalty\ .

Consider the decomposition

\displaystyle P\left\{G_{n}\geq t^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{G_{n}\geq t^{\prime},|Q_{n}-n/2|\leq\delta\frac{n}{2}\right\}+P\left\{|Q_{n}-n/2|\geq\delta\frac{n}{2}\right\}

(B.116)

\displaystyle\leq P\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq\delta\frac{n}{2}}\Big\|\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)};D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}\geq t^{\prime}\right\}+P\left\{|Q_{n}-n/2|\geq\delta\frac{n}{2}\right\},

for some
\delta>0
to be chosen. Observe that

(B.117)

P\left\{|Q_{n}-n/2|\geq\frac{\delta n}{2}\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{6}\right)

by the multiplicative Chernoff bound. To bound the first term in (
B.116
), we combine
Lemma

A.5
with the following Lévy type inequality for independent random vectors, due to
Montgomery-Smith, (1993)
.

Lemma B.3

(Theorem 1.1.5,
De la Pena and Giné, (1999)
)
.

Let
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
be independent random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. There exist universal constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
such that

(B.118)

P\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}Z_{i}\|_{\infty}>t\right\}\leq C_{1}P\left\{\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\|_{\infty}>\frac{t}{C_{2}}\right\}

for all
t>0
.

In particular, as

\|\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\hat{u}(x^{(j)};D_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\penalty\ ,

and

(B.119)

\max_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k=1}^{\delta n/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\hat{u}(x^{(j)};D_{i})\right)^{2}\right]=2\delta\penalty\ ,

Lemma

A.5
and
Lemma

B.3
imply that

(B.120)

\displaystyle P\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq\delta\frac{n}{2}}\Big\|\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\hat{u}\left(\bm{x}^{(d)};D_{i}\right)\Big\|_{\infty}\geq C\left(\delta\log^{1/2}(dn)+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\log^{2}(dn)\right)\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\penalty\ .

Now, the choice

(B.121)

\delta=C\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n}}

gives

(B.122)

P\left\{|Q_{n}-n/2|\geq\frac{\delta n}{2}\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}

by (
B.117
). Plugging this choice into (
B.120
) yields

(B.123)

\displaystyle P\left\{\max_{1\leq k\leq\delta\frac{n}{2}}\Big\|\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\hat{u}\left(\bm{x}^{(d)};D_{i}\right)\Big\|_{\infty}\geq C\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\log^{2}(dn)\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}\penalty\ .

Hence, we find that the inequality

\displaystyle P\bigg\{\Big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(V_{i}-\tilde{V}_{i}\right)\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\Big\|_{\infty}

(B.124)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\geq C\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{4}\left(dn\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/2}\bigg\}\lesssim\frac{1}{n}

holds for all
n
sufficiently large.

Thus, by setting

t^{\prime}=C\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{4}\left(dn\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/2}\penalty\ ,

we find that

\displaystyle P\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\hat{u}(\bm{x}^{(d)},D_{i})\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{\Lambda^{-1/2}Z\in\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\right\}

(B.125)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\sqrt{\log(d)}\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{4}\left(dn\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/2}\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}\left(dn\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}\penalty\ ,

with probability greater than
1-Cn^{-1/2}\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\varphi\log^{3/2}(dn)
. Putting the pieces together, the inequalities (
B.104
) and (
B.125
) imply that

\displaystyle P\left\{\sqrt{n}R_{n}^{*}(\bm{x}^{(d)})\in\mathsf{R}\mid\mathbf{D}_{n}\right\}-P\left\{Z\in\mathsf{R}\right\}\lesssim\left(\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{5}\left(dn\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\right)^{1/4}+\delta_{n}\log^{5/2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C(n^{-1/2}\varphi\underline{\lambda}^{-1}\log^{3/2}(dn)+(nd)^{-1})
, as required.

B.3.
Proof of
Lemma

B.1

Again, we take
\theta_{0}(x)=0
for all
x
, without loss of generality. Recall from the proof of
Theorem

B.2
, Part (ii), that

V_{i}
is a random variable taking the value
1
when
i

is an element of the subset
\mathsf{h}
, and taking the value
-1
otherwise, and that

(B.126)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})\penalty\ .

Define the objects

(B.127)

\displaystyle T_{n}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})-W_{n}(x)\quad\text{and}\quad T_{\mathsf{h}}(x)=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i\in\mathsf{h}}\overline{u}(x,D_{i})-W_{\mathsf{h}}(x)\penalty\ .

and recall the objects
\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})
and
\Delta_{n}(x^{(j)})
introduced in (
B.85
) and (
B.100
), respectively.

Observe that we can write

(B.128)

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}^{2}_{n,j}

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\left(R^{*}_{n}(x^{(j)})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-T_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})+T_{n}(x^{(j)})+\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})-\Delta_{n}(x^{(j)})\right)^{2}\right]\penalty\ ,

where the notation
\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\cdot\right]
denotes that the expectation is evaluated only over the random variables
V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}
. Thus, by defining the quantities

(B.129)

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}^{2}_{n,j}

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right)^{2}\right]\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n,j}

\displaystyle=n\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\left(T_{n}(x^{(j)})-T_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})+\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})-\Delta_{n}(x^{(j)})\right)^{2}\right]

we obtain the bound

(B.130)

\displaystyle|\hat{\lambda}^{2}_{n,j}-\lambda^{2}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq|\overline{\lambda}_{n,j}^{2}-\lambda^{2}_{j}|+2\overline{\lambda}_{n,j}\Gamma^{1/2}_{n,j}+\Gamma_{n,j}

by expanding the square and applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Now, we obtain a large deviation bound for
\Gamma_{n,j}
. Observe that
Assumption

B.3
and (
B.82
) imply that

(B.131)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}T_{n}(x^{(j)})^{2}\right)^{q/2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{2q}\delta_{n}^{q}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\Delta_{n}(x^{(j)})^{2}\right)^{q/2}\right]\lesssim q^{2q}\delta_{n}^{q}\penalty\ .

respectively. Likewise, Jensen’s inequality implies that

(B.132)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}T_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})^{2}\right]\right)^{q/2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\left|\sqrt{\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}}T_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})\right|^{q}\right]\right]\lesssim q^{2q}\delta_{n}^{q}

and analogously

(B.133)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})^{2}\right]\right)^{q/2}\right]\lesssim q^{2q}\delta_{n}^{q}\penalty\ .

Thus, by taking
q=2\vee 2\log(nd)
and applying Markov and a union bound, we find that

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\frac{n}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[T_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{V}\left[\Delta_{\mathsf{h}}(x^{(j)})^{2}\right]

(B.134)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+T_{n}(x^{(j)})^{2}+\Delta_{n}(x^{(j)})^{2}\bigg)\lesssim\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}

with probability at least
1-C/nd
. Consequently, we find that

(B.135)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\Gamma_{n,j}\lesssim\underline{\lambda}^{2}\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}

with probability at least
1-C/nd
.

Next, we derive a large deviation bound for the difference
|\overline{\lambda}_{n,j}^{2}-\lambda^{2}_{j}|
. Observe that we can evaluate

(B.136)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[V_{i}V_{i^{\prime}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[V_{i}\mid V_{i^{\prime}}=1\right]-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[V_{i^{\prime}}\mid V_{i}=-1\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{n/2-1}{n-1}-\frac{n/2}{n-1}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{n/2}{n-1}-\frac{n/2-1}{n-1}\right)=-\frac{1}{n-1}

and thereby

\displaystyle\overline{\lambda}^{2}_{n,j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\mathbb{E}\left[V_{i}V_{i^{\prime}}\right]\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i^{\prime}})

(B.137)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i^{\prime}})\penalty\ .

We now bound the two terms appearing in (
B.137
). First, we apply the following
Rosenthal type inequality.

Lemma B.4

(
Boucheron et al.,, 2005
, Theorem 9,
)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{b}
be independent
centered random variables. For any integer
q\geq 2
, we have that

\|\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}X_{i}\|_{q}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\Var(X_{i})q}{b}}+\frac{q}{b}\|\max_{i\in[b]}|X_{i}|\|_{q}.

In particular, the variables

(B.138)

\displaystyle\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-\lambda_{j}^{2}

are independent and mean-zero. Moreover,
Assumption

B.3
implies that

(B.139)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-\lambda_{j}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{u}^{4}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\right]\leq\lambda_{j}^{2}\varphi^{2}\penalty\ .

In turn, the bound
\|\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\varphi
gives

(B.140)

\displaystyle\left\|\max_{i\in[n]}\left|\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-\lambda_{j}^{2}\right|\right\|_{q}\lesssim q^{2}\varphi^{2}

for every
q\geq 2\vee 2\log(nd)
. Therefore,
Lemma

B.4
implies that

(B.141)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-\lambda_{j}^{2}\right)\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{j}\varphi\sqrt{\frac{q}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}q^{3}}{n}\penalty\ .

By taking
q=2\vee 2\log(nd)
, applying Markov and a union bound gives

(B.142)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\overline{u}^{2}(x^{(j)},D_{i})-\lambda_{j}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
.

To handle the second term, we apply the following sub-exponential formulation of the
Hanson and Wright, (1971)
concentration inequality for quadratic forms, due to
Götze et al., (2021)
.

Lemma B.5

(Proposition 1.1,
Götze et al., (2021)
)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be independent, centered, random variables satisfying
\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{2}\right]=\sigma_{i}^{2}
and
\|X_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\varphi
. If
A=(a_{i,i^{\prime}})
is any symmetric
n\times n
matrix, then the inequality

\displaystyle P\left\{\big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}a_{i,i^{\prime}}X_{i}X_{i^{\prime}}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}^{2}a_{i,i}\big|\geq t\right\}

(B.143)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{1}{C}\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\varphi^{4}\|A\|^{2}_{F}},\frac{t^{1/2}}{\varphi\|A\|_{\mathsf{op}}^{1/2}}\right)\right)

holds for any
t\geq 0
, where
\|\cdot\|_{F}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\mathsf{op}}
denote the Frobenius and
\ell_{2}
operator norms, respectively.

In particular, let
A
denote the
n\times n
matrix with zeroes on the diagonal and
(n(n-1))^{-1}
in every other entry. In this case

(B.144)

\displaystyle\|A\|^{2}_{F}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{n-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\|A\|^{1/2}_{\mathsf{op}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}

and so
Lemma

B.5
implies that

(B.145)

\sup_{j\in[d]}\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\neq i}\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i})\overline{u}(x^{(j)},D_{i^{\prime}})\Big|\lesssim\frac{\varphi^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}\log^{2}(dn)

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
.

Hence, by combining (
B.137
), (
B.142
), and (
B.145
), we find that

(B.146)

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\left|\frac{\overline{\lambda}_{n,j}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
. Thus, by plugging (
B.135
) and (
B.146
) into the bound (
B.130
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{j\in[d]}\left|\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{n,j}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{j\in[d]}\left|\frac{\overline{\lambda}_{n,j}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}-1\right|+2\sup_{j\in[d]}\frac{\overline{\lambda}_{n,j}}{\lambda_{j}}\sup_{j\in[d]}\frac{\Gamma_{n,j}^{1/2}}{\lambda_{j}}+\sup_{j\in[d]}\frac{\Gamma_{n,j}}{\lambda_{j}^{2}}

(B.147)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varphi}{\underline{\lambda}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(dn)}{n}}+\frac{\varphi^{2}\log^{3}(dn)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}n}+\log^{2}(dn)\delta_{n}+\log^{4}(dn)\delta_{n}^{2}

with probability greater than
1-C/nd
, as required.

Appendix C
Proof of
Theorem

3.1

C.1.
Proof of
Theorem

3.1

The result follows from an application of
Theorem

B.1
