where the first inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality, the second inequality follows from Honesty, i.e.,
Assumption

3.1
, Part (i), and the final inequality follows from
Assumption

D.1
,
Assumption

D.2
, and
Assumption

3.1
, Part (ii).

To handle the second term (
E.21
), let
\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\prime\prime}=(D_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{i=1}^{n}
be another independent copy of
\mathbf{D}_{n}
. By Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{1}}\left(A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{1})})-A(x;g_{0})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{1}}\kappa(x,X_{i}^{\prime\prime};D_{\mathsf{s}_{1}}^{\prime\prime})\left(m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{1})})-m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},g_{0})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

(E.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{1})}-g_{0}\|_{q,\infty}^{q}\right]+\varepsilon_{b}^{q/2},

where the final inequality follows from the same argument used to obtain (
E.22
). Hence, the bounds (
E.22
) and (
E.23
) imply that

\displaystyle\|F_{3}(x;\mathsf{s}_{1})\|_{q}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(q^{q}\left(\frac{b}{n}\right)^{q/4}\delta_{n,g}^{q}+\varepsilon_{b}^{q/2}\right)^{2/q}

(E.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim q^{2}\left(\left(\frac{b}{n}\right)^{1/2}\delta_{n,g}^{2}+\varepsilon_{b}\right),

where we have used the rate condition (
D.7
) and the fact that
\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{1})}
and
\hat{g}_{n}
are identically distributed.

Next, consider the term (
E.19
). For
r\neq l
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|F_{3}(x;\mathsf{s}_{r})-F_{3}^{(-l)}(x;\mathsf{s}_{r})\right|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\left(a(x;D_{i},D_{\mathsf{s}_{r}},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-a(x;D_{i},D_{\mathsf{s}_{r}},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.-\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\left(A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

(E.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\left(a(x;D_{i},D_{\mathsf{s}_{r}},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-a(x;D_{i},D_{\mathsf{s}_{r}},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

(E.26)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\left(A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

by the triangle inequality. To bound the quantity (
E.25
), observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\kappa(x,X_{i};D_{\mathsf{s}_{r}})\left(m(D_{i},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-m(D_{i},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i\in[b]}\left|m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n})-m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-[b])})\right|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq b\mathbb{E}\left[\left|m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n})-m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-[b])})\right|^{q}\right]

(E.27)

\displaystyle\lesssim b\left(qn^{-1}b\gamma(q)\right)^{q},

where the first inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality,
Assumption

3.1
, Part (ii), and exchangeability, and the final inequality follows from the independent-copy stability bound in
Assumption

D.3
.

Similarly, to bound the quantity (
E.26
), let
\mathbf{D}_{n}^{\prime\prime}
be an independent copy of
\mathbf{D}_{n}
. By Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\left(A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-A(x;\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathsf{s}_{r}}\kappa(x,X_{i}^{\prime\prime};D_{\mathsf{s}_{r}}^{\prime\prime})\left(m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r})})-m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-\mathsf{s}_{r},-\mathsf{s}_{l})})\right)\bigg|^{q}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i\in[b]}\left|m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n})-m(D_{i}^{\prime\prime},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-[b])})\right|^{q}\right]

(E.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim b\left(qn^{-1}b\gamma(q)\right)^{q},

where the final inequality again follows from the independent-copy stability bound in
Assumption

D.3
. Consequently, (
E.27
) and (
E.28
) imply that

(E.29)

\displaystyle\|F_{3}(x;\mathsf{s}_{r})-F_{3}^{(-l)}(x;\mathsf{s}_{r})\|_{q}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim b^{2/q}\left(qn^{-1}b\gamma(q)\right)^{2}\penalty\ .

Putting the pieces together, (
E.17
), (
E.24
), and (
E.29
) imply that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{l=1}^{k}\left(\frac{1}{k}\left(F_{3}(x;\mathsf{s}_{l})-F_{3}^{(-l)}(x;\mathsf{s}_{l})\right)+\frac{1}{k}\sum_{r\neq l}\left(F_{3}(x;\mathsf{s}_{r})-F_{3}^{(-l)}(x;\mathsf{s}_{r})\right)\right)^{2}\bigg\|_{q/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\left[\frac{q^{2}}{k}\left(\left(\frac{b}{n}\right)^{1/2}\delta_{n,g}^{2}+\varepsilon_{b}\right)+kb^{2/q}\left(qn^{-1}b\gamma(q)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{1}{k}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)^{1/4}\delta_{n,g}+\varepsilon_{b}^{1/2}+b^{1/q}\gamma(q)\right)q\penalty\ ,

where the final inequality uses
k=\lfloor n/b\rfloor
.
\blacksquare

E.3.
Proof of
Lemma

D.1

Consider the decomposition

\displaystyle m(D_{i},\hat{g}_{n})-m(D_{i},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-[b])})

\displaystyle=\left(\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},1)-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},1)\right)-\left(\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},0)-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},0)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(-[b])}(W_{i},Z_{i})\left(\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},W_{i})-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},W_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},W_{i})\right)\left(\hat{\beta}_{n}(W_{i},Z_{i})-\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(-[b])}(W_{i},Z_{i})\right).

We have that

\displaystyle\|m(D_{i},\hat{g}_{n})-m(D_{i},\hat{g}_{n}^{(-[b])})\|_{2p}

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},1)-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},1)\|_{2p}+\|\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},0)-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},0)\|_{2p}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(-[b])}(W_{i},Z_{i})(\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},W_{i})-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},W_{i}))\|_{2p}

\displaystyle\quad+\|(Y_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},W_{i}))(\hat{\beta}_{n}(W_{i},Z_{i})-\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(-[b])}(W_{i},Z_{i}))\|_{2p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},1)-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},1)\|_{2p}+\|\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},0)-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},0)\|_{2p}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\hat{\mu}_{n}(Z_{i},W_{i})-\hat{\mu}_{n}^{(-[b])}(Z_{i},W_{i})\|_{2p}+\|\hat{\beta}_{n}(W_{i},Z_{i})-\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(-[b])}(W_{i},Z_{i})\|_{2p},

where the first inequality follows from the triangle inequality and
the second inequality follows from the boundedness condition (
D.12
), applied to both the original and resampled nuisance estimators. This verifies the condition (
D.13
). An identical argument verifies the condition (
D.14
).

\blacksquare

E.4.
Proof of
Lemma

D.2

To ease notation, we omit dependence on the evaluation point
z
.
Recall the definition of the Hájek projection

u^{(1)}(D)=\mathbb{E}\left[u(D_{[b^{\prime}]})\mid D_{1}=D\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[u(D_{[b^{\prime}]})\right],

where
D
is an independent copy of
D_{1}
. Let

\displaystyle\nu^{2}

\displaystyle=\Var(u(D_{[b^{\prime}]}))\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{b^{\prime}}^{2}=\Var(u^{(1)}(D))

denote the kernel variance and Hájek projection variance, respectively. Consider the decomposition

(E.30)

\displaystyle\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n})-\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n}^{(-[b])})

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left(u^{(1)}(D_{i})-u^{(1)}(D_{i}^{\prime})\right)

(E.31)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b^{\prime}]})]-\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)

(E.32)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n}^{(-[b])})-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b^{\prime}]})]-\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{b}u^{(1)}(D_{i}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=b+1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)\right)\penalty\ .

The result is obtained by giving higher-order moment bounds for each
of the terms (
E.30
), (
E.31
),
and (
E.32
).

We begin by considering the terms (
E.31
) and
(
E.32
). Observe that the decomposition (
2.5
),
Lemma

2.2
, and the triangle inequality imply that

(E.33)

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n})-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b^{\prime}]})]-\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle\leq\phi q\sum_{m=2}^{b^{\prime}}\left(\frac{Cqb^{\prime}}{n}\right)^{m/2}\lesssim\phi q^{2}\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}

for
q\leq cn/b^{\prime}
. Likewise,

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n}^{(-[b])})-\mathbb{E}[u(D_{[b^{\prime}]})]-\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{b}u^{(1)}(D_{i}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=b+1}^{n}u^{(1)}(D_{i})\right)\right\|_{q}

(E.34)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\phi q\sum_{m=2}^{b^{\prime}}\left(\frac{Cqb^{\prime}}{n}\right)^{m/2}\lesssim\phi q^{2}\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\penalty\ .

To handle the term (
E.30
), we apply
Lemma

B.4
. In particular, Hoeffding orthogonality gives

b^{\prime}\sigma_{b^{\prime}}^{2}\leq\Var(u(D_{[b^{\prime}]}))\lesssim\phi^{2},

and so
\sigma_{b^{\prime}}^{2}\lesssim\phi^{2}/b^{\prime}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left(u^{(1)}(D_{i})-u^{(1)}(D_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\|_{q}

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{\prime}b}{n}\left\|\frac{1}{b}\sum_{i=1}^{b}\left(u^{(1)}(D_{i})-u^{(1)}(D_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\|_{q}

(E.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{b^{\prime}b}{n}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{b^{\prime}}^{2}q}{b}}+q\frac{\phi}{b}\right)\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{b^{\prime}b}}{n}q\phi+\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}q\phi\penalty\ .

Putting the pieces together, we find that

\|\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n})-\hat{g}_{n}(\mathbf{D}_{n}^{(-[b])})\|_{q}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{b^{\prime}b}}{n}q\phi+\frac{b^{\prime}}{n}q^{2}\phi

by combining the bounds (
E.33
), (
E.34
),
and (
E.35
), as required.

\blacksquare

Validation

Notes:
Figure

F.2
displays scatterplots comparing the moments of the data from
Banerjee et al., (2015)
to the GAN-generated simulation data. Columns differentiate between different types of variables. Rows differentiate between different types of moments. The x-axis of each sub-panel measures the moments of the true data. The y-axis of each sub-panel measures the moments of the generated data. The x and y axes in the first two rows are displayed in log-scale. A forty-five degree line is displayed in all sub-panels. Blue and green dots denote moments conditioned on treatment being set to one and zero, respectively. Black dots denote unconditioned moments.

Panel B displays a heat-map giving the density of the joint distribution of baseline consumption and baseline assets associated with our calibrated simulation.
